6

Proof A.3
.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)=x
. There are three cases to be analyzed:

(1)

M=x
.

This case follows trivially. First,
x:\sigma\models x:\sigma
and
\Gamma=\emptyset
. Second,
x{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=N
, by definition. Since
\Delta\models N:\sigma
, by hypothesis, the result follows.

(2)

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

In this case,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x
, and by inversion of the typing derivation one has the following derivation:

\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma^{m}\models M^{\prime}:\delta^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Gamma_{2}\models B:\delta^{l}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

(\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma^{m})\wedge\Gamma_{2}\models M^{\prime}B:\tau

where
\Gamma,x:\sigma^{k}=(\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma^{m})\wedge\Gamma_{2}
,
\delta
is a strict type, and
j,l,m
are non-negative integers, possibly different with
m\geq 1
.

By IH, we get
\Gamma_{1}\wedge\Delta,x:\sigma^{m-1}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\delta^{j}\rightarrow\tau
, which gives the following derivation:

\Gamma_{1}\wedge\Delta,x:\sigma^{m-1}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\delta^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Gamma_{2}\models B:\delta^{l}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

(\Gamma_{1}\wedge\Delta,x:\sigma^{m-1})\wedge\Gamma_{2}\models(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})B:\tau

Therefore, from Def.
2.2
, one has
\Gamma\wedge\Delta,x:\sigma^{k-1}\models(M^{\prime}B){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau
, and the result follows.

(3)

M=M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

In this case,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x
, with
x\not=y
, and by inversion of the typing derivation one has the following derivation:

\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{l},x:\sigma^{m}\models M^{\prime}:\tau

\Gamma_{2}\models B:\delta^{j}

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

(\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma^{m})\wedge\Gamma_{2}\models M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma,x:\sigma^{k}=(\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma^{m})\wedge\Gamma_{2}
,
\delta
is a strict type and
j,l,m
are positive integers with
m\geq 1
.
By IH, we get
(\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{l},x:\sigma^{m-1})\wedge\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau
and

(\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{l},x:\sigma^{m-1})\wedge\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau

\Gamma_{2}\models B:\delta^{j}

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

(\Gamma_{1},{y}:\delta^{l},x:\sigma^{m-1})\wedge\Delta\wedge\Gamma_{2}\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

From Def.
2.2
,
M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
, therefore,
\Gamma\wedge\Delta,x:\sigma^{k-1}\models(M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau
and the result follows.

See
7

Proof A.4
.

By structural induction on the reduction rules. We proceed by analysing the rule applied in
\mathbb{M}
. There are seven cases:

(1)

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=(\lambda x.M)B\longrightarrow M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle=\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
.

Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
, by inversion of the typing derivation one has the following derivation:

\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}abs]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\models\lambda x.M:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models(\lambda x.M)B:\tau

for
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta
. Notice that

\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{j}\models M:\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Therefore,
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime}:\tau
and the result follows.

(2)

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, where
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag
,
k\geq 1
,
\#(x,M)=k
, and
\mathsf{head}(M)=x
. The reduction is as follows:

\mathsf{head}(M)=x

B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag\ ,\ k\geq 1

\#(x,M)=k

\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}

M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\rightarrow M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(B\setminus\!\!\setminus N_{1})$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+\cdots+M{\{\!|}N_{k}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$(B\setminus\!\!\setminus N_{k})$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

To simplify the proof we take
k=2
, as the case
k>2
is similar. Therefore, by inversion of the typing derivation and
B=\Lbag N_{1},N_{2}\Rbag
:

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma\wedge\sigma\models M:\tau

\Delta_{1}\models N_{1}:\sigma

\Delta_{2}\models N_{2}:\sigma

​​
\mathtt{[F{:}\mathtt{1}]}

\models\mathtt{1}:\omega

​​
\mathtt{[F{:}bag]}

\Delta_{2}\models\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag:\sigma

​​
\mathtt{[F{:}bag]}

\Delta\models B:\sigma\wedge\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Delta=\Delta_{1}\wedge\Delta_{2}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta
. By the Substitution Lemma (Lemma
6
), there exists a derivation
\Pi_{1}
of
(\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma)\wedge\Delta_{1}\models M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}:\tau
and a derivation
\Pi_{2}
of
(\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma)\wedge\Delta_{2}\models M{\{\!|}N_{2}/x{|\!\}}:\tau
. Therefore, one has the following derivation:

\Pi_{1}

\Delta_{2}\models\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau\

\Pi_{2}

\Delta_{1}\models\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N_{2}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau\

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+M{\{\!|}N_{2}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Assuming
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}={\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+M{\{\!|}N_{2}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, the result follows.

(3)

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Fail]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots\cdot,N_{k}\Rbag\ ,\ k\geq 0
,
\#(x,M)\not=k
and we can perform the following reduction:

\#(x,M)\neq\mathsf{size}(B)

\widetilde{y}=(\mathsf{mfv}(M)\setminus x)\uplus\mathsf{mfv}(B)

\mathtt{[R:Fail]}

M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

with
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
. By hypothesis, one has the derivation:

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{j}

\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{k}\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Notice that we also have from
\#(x,M)\neq\mathsf{size}(B)
that
j\neq k
.Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta
and we may type the following:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\cdots

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Gamma\models\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

(4)

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B
where
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag
,
k\geq 0
and we can perform the following reduction:

\mathsf{size}(B)=k

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{mfv}(B)

\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}

where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}
. By hypothesis and inversion of the typing derivation, there exists the following derivation:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}:\pi^{\prime}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\pi

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B:\tau

Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta
and we may type the following:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\cdots

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Gamma\models\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

(5)

Rule
\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag
,
k\geq 1
and
we can perform the following reduction:

\mathsf{size}(B)=k

\#(x,\widetilde{z})+k\not=0

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{mfv}(B)

\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}

where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}
. By hypothesis and inversion of the typing derivation, there exists a derivation:

\mathtt{dom}((\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{k})^{\dagger})=\widetilde{z}

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

\Gamma^{\prime},{x}:\sigma^{k}\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}:\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{j}

\mathtt{[F{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta
and we may type the following:

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

{\Gamma}\models\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\cdots

\mathtt{[F{:}fail]}

{\Gamma}\models\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

{\Gamma}\models\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{z}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{y}}:\tau

(6)

Rule
\mathtt{[R:TCont]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=C[M]
and the reduction is as follows:

M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}_{1}+\cdots+M^{\prime}_{l}

\mathtt{[R:TCont]}

C[M]\longrightarrow C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{l}]

where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{l}]
.
The proof proceeds by analysing the context
C
:

(1)

C=[\cdot]\ B
.

In this case
\mathbb{M}=M\ B
, for some
B
, and the following derivation holds:

\Gamma^{\prime}\models M:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models M\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta
.

Since
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
and
M\longrightarrow M_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots+M_{l}^{\prime}
, it follows by IH that
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots+M_{l}^{\prime}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
. By applying
\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}
, one has
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{i}^{\prime}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
, for
i=1,\ldots,l
. Therefore, we may type the following:

\forall i\in{1,\cdots,l}

\Gamma^{\prime}\models M^{\prime}_{i}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F{:}app]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models(M^{\prime}_{i}\ B):\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\models(M^{\prime}_{1}\ B)+\cdots+(M^{\prime}_{l}\ B):\tau

Thus,
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M^{\prime}}:\tau
, and the result follows.

(2)

C=([\cdot])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

This case is similar to the previous one.

(7)

Rule
\mathtt{[R:ECont]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}]
where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}
then we can perform the following reduction:

\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}

\mathtt{[R:ECont]}

D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}]\longrightarrow D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}]

Hence
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}]
.
The proof proceeds by analysing the context
D
:

(1)

D=[\cdot]+\mathbb{N}
. In this case
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}+\mathbb{N}
by inversion of the typing derivation:

\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}:\tau

\Gamma\models\mathbb{N}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}+\mathbb{N}:\tau

Since
\Gamma\vdash\mathbb{M}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}:\tau
and
\mathbb{M}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}
, by IH, it follows that
\Gamma\vdash\mathbb{M}^{{}^{\prime\prime\prime}}:\tau
and we may type the following:

\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}:\tau

\Gamma\models\mathbb{N}:\tau

\mathtt{[F{:}sum]}

\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\mathbb{N}:\tau

Therefore,
\Gamma\vdash\mathbb{M^{\prime}}:\tau
and the result follows.

(2)

D=\mathbb{N}+[\cdot]
.
This case is similar to the previous one.

Appendix B
Appendix to
\lx@sectionsign

3.3

See
11

Proof B.1
.

By structural induction on the reduction rules. We will consider two key reduction rules, the other cases follow analogously via application of the IH.

(1)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}
. In this case, we have

B=\Lbag M_{1}\Rbag\cdots\Lbag M_{k}\Rbag\qquad k\geq 1

M\not=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}

\!M[x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}

Notice that if a bag is consistent then each element in the bag is consistent, that is, for any permutation
B_{i}
of the bag
B
then each
B_{i}(n)
is consistent. Then, the assumption of consistency for
(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, along with each element of the bag being consistent implies consistency of
\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}
for each permutation of
B
.

(2)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Lin\text{-}Fetch]}
.
In this case, we have

\mathsf{head}(M)=x

\mathtt{[RS{:}Lin\text{-}Fetch]}

M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}

This case follows from the fact that
M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}
preserves consistency. The argument is by structural induction, with base case of
M=x
together with the fact that
N
is consistent trivially implies that
x{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}
must also be consistent. As for the inductive step, notice that ‘adding’
N
to the structure of
M
does not break any of the consistency requirements: the consistency of
M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
implies that the free variables of
M
and
N
are disjoint.

See
13

Proof B.2
.

By induction on the structure of
M
:

(1)

When
M=x
then we have
x{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=N
and may derive the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash N:\tau
with
x\not\in\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)
. By taking
\Gamma_{1}=\emptyset
and
\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma
as
\Gamma=\emptyset\wedge\Gamma
the case follows as
\Gamma\vdash N:\tau
and

\mathtt{[TS:var]}

x:\sigma\vdash x:\sigma

(2)

When
M=(M\ B)
then we have that
(M\ B){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ B
. Let us consider two cases:

(I)

When
x\in\mathsf{fv}(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1}\vdash M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\pi\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}

\Delta\vdash B:\pi

\mathtt{[TS:app]}

(\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1})\wedge\Delta\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ B:\tau^{\prime}

By the IH we have that
\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1}\vdash M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\pi\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
implies that
\exists\ \Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k}\vdash M:\tau
, and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\Gamma_{2}
.

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k}\vdash M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\pi\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}

\Delta\vdash B:\pi

\mathtt{[TS:app]}

(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k})\wedge\Delta\vdash M\ B:\tau^{\prime}

(II)

When
x\not\in\mathsf{fv}(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\pi\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}

\Delta\vdash B:\pi

\mathtt{[TS:app]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ B:\tau^{\prime}

By the IH we have that
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\pi\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}
implies that
\exists\ \Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma\vdash M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau
, and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\Gamma_{2}
.

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma\vdash M:\pi\rightarrow\tau^{\prime}

\Delta\vdash B:\pi

\mathtt{[TS:app]}

(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma)\wedge\Delta\vdash M\ B:\tau^{\prime}

(3)

When
M=M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
then we have that
(M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ [\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
x\not=y
.

(I)

When
x\in\mathsf{fv}(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})
:

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{y}:\delta^{j}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

\Delta\vdash B:\delta^{j}

\mathtt{[TS:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

By the IH we have that
\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau
implies that
\exists\ \Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash M:\tau
, and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{j}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{j})\wedge\Gamma_{2}
.

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma^{k},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma^{k},{y}:\delta^{j}\vdash M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

\Delta\vdash B:\delta^{j}

\mathtt{[TS:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1}\wedge\Delta\vdash(M)[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

(II)

When
x\not\in\mathsf{fv}(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})
:

\Gamma^{\prime},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{k}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

\Delta\vdash B:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[TS:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

By the IH we have that
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau
implies that
\exists\ \Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma,{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{k}\vdash M:\tau
, and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k})\wedge\Gamma_{2}
.

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma,{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{k}\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma,{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

\Delta\vdash B:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[TS:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\wedge\Delta\vdash(M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

(4)

When
M=M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]
then we have that
(M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ [\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]
where
x\not=y
.

(I)

When
x\in\mathsf{fv}(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})
:

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{y}:\delta^{j}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

By the IH we have that
\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau
implies that
\exists\ \Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash M:\tau
, and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{j}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{j})\wedge\Gamma_{2}
.

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma^{k},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma^{k},{y}:\delta^{j}\vdash M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

(II)

When
x\not\in\mathsf{fv}(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})
:

\Gamma^{\prime},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{k}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

By the IH we have that
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau
implies that
\exists\ \Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma,{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{k}\vdash M:\tau
, and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k})\wedge\Gamma_{2}
.

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma,{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{k}\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma,{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

(5)

When
M=M{\langle\!|}N/y{|\!\rangle}
then we have that
(M{\langle\!|}N/y{|\!\rangle}){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})\ {\langle\!|}N/y{|\!\rangle}
where
x\not=y
.

(I)

When
x\in\mathsf{fv}(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})
:

\Delta\vdash N:\delta\qquad\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{y}:\delta\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau

\mathtt{[TS\!:\!ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{y}:\delta,\Delta\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}){\langle\!|}N/y{|\!\rangle}:\tau

By the IH we have that
\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k-1},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau
implies that
\exists\ \Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k},{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{j}\vdash M:\tau
, and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{j}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{j})\wedge\Gamma_{2}
.

\Delta\vdash N:\delta\qquad\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma^{k},{y}:\delta\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[TS\!:\!ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma^{k},{y}:\delta,\Delta\vdash M{\langle\!|}N/y{|\!\rangle}:\tau

(II)

When
x\not\in\mathsf{fv}(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})
:

\Delta\vdash N:\delta\qquad\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau

\mathtt{[TS\!:\!ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta,\Delta\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}){\langle\!|}N/y{|\!\rangle}:\tau

By the IH we have that
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}\vdash(M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}):\tau
implies that
\exists\ \Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
such that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:\sigma,{\widetilde{y}}:\delta^{k}\vdash M:\tau
, and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash N:\sigma
with
\Gamma^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{y}:\delta^{k})\wedge\Gamma_{2}
.

\Delta\vdash N:\delta\qquad\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma,{y}:\delta\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[TS\!:\!ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime}_{1},x:\sigma,{y}:\delta,\Delta\vdash M{\langle\!|}N/y{|\!\rangle}:\tau

(6)

When
M=\lambda y.M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]
then linear head substitution is undefined on this term as
\mathsf{head}(M)\not=x
.

(7)

When
M=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}
then
M
is not well typed.

See
14

Proof B.3
.

By induction on the reduction rule applied.
There are five possible cases.

(1)

When
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
is reduced to via the Rule
\mathtt{[RS:Beta]}
:

\mathtt{[RS:Beta]}

(\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B\longrightarrow M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

Then
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
can be type as followed:

{\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{k}\vdash M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\tau}

\Delta\vdash B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[TS:ex\text{-}sub]}

{\Gamma\wedge\Delta\vdash M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau}

From the typing of
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
we can deduce that
\mathbb{M}=(\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B
may be typed by:

{\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{k}\vdash M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\tau}

\mathtt{[TS:abs]}

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\sigma^{k}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\vdash B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[TS:app]}

{\Gamma\wedge\Delta\vdash(\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B:\tau}

(2)

When
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
is reduced to via the Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}
:

B=\Lbag M_{1}\Rbag\cdots\Lbag M_{k}\Rbag\qquad k\geq 1

M\not=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}

\!M[x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}

Then
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\!M[x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
can be type as followed:

\Gamma,x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{k}:\sigma\vdash M:\tau

\Delta_{1}\vdash B_{i}(1):\sigma

\vdots

\Delta_{k}\vdash B_{i}(k):\sigma

\mathtt{[TS\!:\!ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma,\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{k}\vdash M{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\forall B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)

\mathtt{[TS{:}sum]}

\Gamma,\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{k}\vdash\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

From the typing of
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
we can deduce that
\mathbb{M}=(\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B
may be typed by:

\Delta_{1}\vdash M_{1}:\sigma

\Delta_{k}\vdash M_{k}:\sigma

\ \vdots

\mathtt{[TS{:}bag]}

\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{k}\vdash B:\sigma^{k}

\Gamma,x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{k}:\sigma\vdash M:\tau

\qquad\Gamma,x:\sigma^{k}\vdash M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[TS\!:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma,\Delta_{1},\cdots,\Delta_{k}\vdash M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

(3)

When
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
is reduced to via the Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Lin\text{-}Fetch]}
:

\mathsf{head}(M)=x

\mathtt{[RS{:}Lin\text{-}Fetch]}

M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}

The result follow from
13
.

(4)

When
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
is reduced to via the Rule
\mathtt{[RS:TCont]}
:

M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}_{1}+\cdots+M^{\prime}_{k}

\mathtt{[RS:TCont]}

C[M]\longrightarrow C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{k}]

Hence the proof follows by the IH on
M
.

(5)

When
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
is reduced to via the Rule
\mathtt{[RS:ECont]}
:

\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime}

\mathtt{[RS:ECont]}

D[\mathbb{M}]\longrightarrow D[\mathbb{M}^{\prime}]

Hence the proof follows by the IH on
M
.

See
15

Proof B.4
.

By structural induction on
M
with
\mathsf{head}(M)=x
.
There are six cases to be analyzed:

(1)

M=x
.

In this case,
x:\sigma\models x:\sigma
and
\Gamma=\emptyset
. Observe that
x{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=N
, since
\Delta\models N:\sigma
, by hypothesis, the result follows.

(2)

M=M^{\prime}\ B
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x
, and the derivation is the following by inversion of the typing derivation:

\Gamma_{1},x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}:\delta^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Gamma_{2}\models B:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
, and
j,k
are non-negative integers, possibly different. Since
\Delta\vdash N:\sigma
, by IH, the result holds for
M^{\prime}
, that is,

\Gamma_{1},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\delta^{j}\rightarrow\tau

which gives the derivation:

\Gamma_{1},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\delta^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Gamma_{2}\models B:\delta^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\models(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})B:\tau

From Def.
3.2
,
(M^{\prime}B){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=(M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}})B
, therefore,
\Gamma,\Delta\models(M^{\prime}B){\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau
and the result follows.

(3)

M=M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x
, for
y\neq x
. Therefore by inversion of the typing derivation,

\Gamma_{1},y_{1}:\delta,\cdots,y_{k}:\delta,x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}:\tau\quad y\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Gamma_{1},y:\delta^{k},x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}[y_{1},\cdots,y_{k}\leftarrow y]:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},y:\delta^{k}
.
By IH, the result follows for
M^{\prime}
, that is,

\Gamma_{1},y_{1}:\delta,\cdots,y_{k}:\delta,\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau

and we have the derivation:

\Gamma_{1},y_{1}:\delta,\cdots,y_{k}:\delta,\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau\quad y\notin\Gamma_{1}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Gamma_{1},y:\delta^{k},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

From Def.
3.2
one has
M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]
. Therefore,
\Gamma,\Delta\models M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau
and the result follows.

(4)

M=M^{\prime}[\leftarrow y]
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[\leftarrow y])=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x
with
x\not=y
,

\Gamma,x:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Gamma,y:\omega,x:\sigma\models M[\leftarrow y]:\tau

and
M^{\prime}[\leftarrow y]{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}[\leftarrow y]
. Then by the IH:

\Gamma,\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Gamma,y:\omega,\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}[\leftarrow y]:\tau

(5)

M=M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y])=x\not=y
by inversion of the typing derivation we have:

\Gamma_{1},\hat{y}:\delta^{k},x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

\Gamma_{2}\models B:\delta^{j}

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

and
M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
. By IH:

\Gamma_{1},\hat{y}:\delta^{k},\Delta\models M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau

\Gamma_{2}\models B:\delta^{j}

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\models M^{\prime}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

(6)

M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/y{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=x\not=y
, by inversion of the typing derivation we have:

\Delta\models M^{\prime\prime}:\delta

\Gamma,y:\delta,x:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},x:\sigma\models M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}:\tau

and
M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}
. Then by the IH:

\Delta\models M^{\prime\prime}:\delta

\Gamma,y:\delta,\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\Delta\models M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}{\langle\!|}M^{\prime\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}:\tau

See
16

Proof B.5
.

By structural induction on the reduction rule from Fig.
5
applied in
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow\mathbb{N}
. There are nine cases to be analyzed:

(1)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=(\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B
and the reduction is:

\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}

(\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B\longrightarrow M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

where
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
we get the following derivation by inversion of the typing derivation:

\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{j}\models M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}abs\text{-}sh]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\models\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models(\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B:\tau

for
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
.
Notice that:

\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{j}\models M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Therefore
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathbb{M}^{\prime}:\tau
and the result follows.

(2)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag
. By inversion of the typing derivation the reduction is:

B=\Lbag N_{1},\cdots,N_{k}\Rbag\quad k\geq 1

M\not=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}

M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M\ {\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}

and
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M\ {\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}.

To simplify the proof we take
k=2
, as the case
k>2
is similar. Therefore,

•

B=\Lbag N_{1},N_{2}\Rbag
; and

•

\mathsf{PER}(B)=\{\Lbag N_{1},N_{2}\Rbag,\Lbag N_{2},N_{1}\Rbag\}

Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
we get a derivation where we first type the bag
B
with the derivation
\Pi
, given next:

\Delta_{1}\models N_{1}:\sigma

\Delta_{2}\models N_{2}:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}\mathtt{1}]}

\models\mathtt{1}:\omega

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag]}

\Delta_{2}\models\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag]}

\Delta\models B:\sigma\wedge\sigma

The full derivation is as follows:

\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,x_{2}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma\wedge\sigma\models M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\Pi

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Delta=\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. We can build a derivation
\Pi_{1,2}
of
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{2}/x_{2}{|\!\rangle}:\tau
as :

\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,x_{2}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\Delta_{1}\models N_{1}:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma,\Delta_{1},x_{2}:\sigma\models M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\Delta_{2}\models N_{2}:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{2}/x_{2}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

Similarly, we can obtain a derivation
\Pi_{2,1}
of
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N_{2}/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{1}/x_{2}{|\!\rangle}:\tau
. Finally, applying Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
:

\Pi_{1,2}

\Pi_{2,1}

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{2}/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}+M{\langle\!|}N_{2}/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{1}/x_{k}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

and the result follows.

(3)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Lin\text{-}Fetch]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\ {\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
where
\mathsf{head}(M)=x
. The reduction is:

\mathsf{head}(M)=x

\mathtt{[RS{:}Lin\text{-}Fetch]}

M\ {\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}

and
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}
. Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
we get the following derivation by inversion of the typing derivation:

\Delta\models N:\sigma

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. By the Substitution Lemma (Lemma
15
), we obtain a derivation
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}:\tau
, and the result follows.

(4)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}TCont]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=C[M]
and the reduction is as follows:

M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}_{1}+\cdots+M^{\prime}_{k}

\mathtt{[RS{:}TCont]}

C[M]\longrightarrow C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{k}]

with
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=C[M^{\prime}_{1}]+\cdots+C[M^{\prime}_{k}]
.
The proof proceeds by analysing the context
C
.

There are four cases:

(1)

C=[\cdot]\ B
.

In this case
\mathbb{M}=M\ B
, for some
B
. Since
\Gamma\vdash\mathbb{M}:\tau
by inversion of the typing derivation, one has the derivation:

\Gamma^{\prime}\models M:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. From
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
and the reduction
M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}_{1}+\cdots+M^{\prime}_{k}
, one has by IH that
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{1}^{\prime}+\ldots,M_{k}^{\prime}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
, which entails
\Gamma^{\prime}\models M_{i}^{\prime}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau
, for
i=1,\ldots,k
, via Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
. Finally, we may type the following:

\forall i\in{1,\cdots,l}

\Gamma^{\prime}\models M^{\prime}_{i}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models(M^{\prime}_{i}\ B):\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models(M^{\prime}_{1}\ B)+\cdots+(M^{\prime}_{l}\ B):\tau

Since
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=(C[M^{\prime}_{1}])+\cdots+(C[M^{\prime}_{l}])=M_{1}^{\prime}B+\ldots+M_{k}^{\prime}B
, the result follows.

(2)

Cases
C=[\cdot]{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
and
C=[\cdot][\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]
are similar to the previous one.

(3)

C=[\cdot][\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

In this case
\mathbb{M}=C[M]=M[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
by inversion of the typing derivation, one has a derivation

\Gamma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS\text{-}weak]}

\Gamma,x:\omega\models M[\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[TS{:}\mathtt{1}]}

\vdash\mathtt{1}:\omega

\mathtt{[FS{:}wf\text{-}bag]}

\models\mathtt{1}:\omega

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma\models M[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

From
M\longrightarrow M_{1}+\ldots+M_{k}
and
\Gamma\models M:\tau
, by the IH, it follows that
\Gamma\models M_{1}+\ldots+M_{k}:\tau
, and consequently,
\Gamma\models M_{i}
, via application of
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
. Therefore, there exists a derivation

\Gamma\models M_{i}:\tau

\Gamma,x:\omega\models M_{i}[\leftarrow x]:\tau

\models\mathtt{1}:\omega

\Gamma\models M_{i}[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

for each
i=1,\ldots,k
. By applying
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
, we obtain
\Gamma\models M_{1}[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle+\ldots+M_{k}[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau
, and the result follows.

(5)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}ECont]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=D[\mathbb{M}_{1}]
where
\mathbb{M}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}_{2}
then we can perform the following reduction:

\mathbb{M}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}_{2}

\mathtt{[RS{:}ECont]}

D[\mathbb{M}_{1}]\longrightarrow D[\mathbb{M}_{2}]

and
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=D[\mathbb{M}_{2}]
.

The proof proceeds by analysing the context
D
. There are two cases:

D=[\cdot]+\mathbb{N}
and
D=\mathbb{N}+[\cdot]
.
We analyze only the first one:

D=[\cdot]+\mathbb{N}
. In this case
\mathbb{M}=\mathbb{M}_{1}+\mathbb{N}
and by inversion of the typing derivation:

\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}_{1}:\tau

\Gamma\models\mathbb{N}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}_{1}+\mathbb{N}:\tau

From
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}_{1}:\tau
and
\mathbb{M}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}_{2}
, by IH, one has that
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}_{2}:\tau
.
Hence we may type the following:

\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}_{2}:\tau

\Gamma\models\mathbb{N}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}_{2}+\mathbb{N}:\tau

Since
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=D[\mathbb{M}_{2}]=\mathbb{M}_{2}+\mathbb{N}
, the result follows.

(6)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
where
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{l}\Rbag
and the reduction is:

k\neq\mathsf{size}(B)

\widetilde{y}=(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\})\cup\mathsf{fv}(B)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail]}

M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

where
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
. Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}
and by inversion of the typing derivation, one has a derivation:

\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\sigma,\ldots,x_{k}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma^{k}\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{j}

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. We may type the following:

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau

since
\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
contain assignments on the free variables in
M
and
B
.
Therefore,
\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau
, by applying
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
, it follows that
\Gamma\models\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}:\tau
, as required.

(7)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B
where
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag
and the reduction is:

B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{fv}(B)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}

and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}
.
Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
and by inversion of the typing derivation, one has the derivation:

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Gamma^{\prime}\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}:\omega\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\pi

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
. After
\mathsf{PER}(B)
applications of
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
, we obtain
\Gamma\models
\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}:\tau
, and the result follows.

(8)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{2}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=(\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
for
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag
and the reduction is:

B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag\quad k+|\widetilde{x}|\neq 0

\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{fv}(B)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{2}]}

(\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}

with
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}
. Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\tau
and by inversion of the typing derivation, one has the derivation:

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Delta,x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}:\tau\quad x\notin\Delta\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Delta,x:\sigma^{j}\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{j}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma,\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

Hence
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta
and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}
and we may type the following:

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Gamma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}:\tau

\cdots

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\Gamma\models\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}:\tau

(9)

Rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{3}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup x}
and the reduction is

\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{fv}(N)

\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{3}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup x}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}

with
\mathbb{M^{\prime}}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}
. Since
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}
and by inversion of the typing derivation, one has the derivation

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Gamma^{\prime},x:\sigma\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup x}:\tau

\Delta\models N:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup x}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}:\tau

where
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
,
\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})=\widetilde{y}
and
\mathtt{dom}(\Delta)=\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{fv}(N)
.

We can type the following:

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}

\Gamma^{\prime},\Delta\models\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\cup\widetilde{z}}:\tau

and the result follows.

See
18

Proof B.6
.

By induction on the typing derivation, with a case analysis on the last applied rule (
Figure

7
). We only consider the cases for the typing rules that relate to the sharing construct and the explicit substitution.
First, consider conditions 1(i) to 1(iv), which are related to
M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]
. The conditions are as follows (i)

\widetilde{x}
contains pairwise distinct variables;
(ii) every
x_{i}\in\widetilde{x}
must occur exactly once in
M
; (iii)
x_{i}
is not a sharing variable;
(iv)
M
is consistent. By considering rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}
, we have:

\Gamma,x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{k}:\sigma\models M:\tau\quad x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Gamma,x:\sigma^{k}\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

Condition 1(i) follows from uniquness of variables within the context. Condition 1(ii) follows from the premise, which ensures that
M
is well-formed with a context including each
x_{i}
; linearity conditions imply that each
x_{i}
must be consumed so it must occur in
M
. Condition 1(iii) also follows directly from the well-formedness of
M
: each
x_{i}
is typed with a strict type, and the rule ensures that the sharing variable
x
is typed with the multiset type
\sigma^{k}
. Finally condition 1(iv) is ensured by the IH.

For conditions 2(i) to 2(iv) which are (i) the variable
x
must occur exactly once in
M
;
(ii)
x
cannot be a sharing variable; (iii)
M
and
N
are consistent; (iv)
\mathsf{fv}(M)\cap\mathsf{fv}(N)=\emptyset
. Consider the case of rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}
:

\Gamma,x:\sigma\models M:\tau\quad\Delta\models N:\sigma

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma,\Delta\models M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}:\tau

First, because
\Gamma
and
\Delta
are disjoint,
x
cannot appear within
\Delta
and
M
must consume the type of
x:\sigma
; hence
x
must occur in
M
, satisfying condition 2(i) and 2(iv).
Second,
\Gamma,x:\sigma
ensures a strict type for
x
;
if
x
were a sharing variable in
M
then
x
would have a multiset type
\pi
. Therefore, condition 2(ii) is satisfied. Finally, condition (iii) is satisfied by induction on
M
and
N
.

See
19

Proof B.7
.

By induction on the structure of
\mathbb{M}
.
Notice that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
ensures consistency for bound variables: it replaces all occurrences of a bound variable (say
y
) with fresh bound variables (say,
y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}
). Thus, the following hold for bound variables: (i) they occur once within a term and (ii) they are not shared themselves, as the sharing of variables only occurs when handling binders associated to explicit substitutions and abstractions.
As for free variables, the translation
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
replaces each occurrence with a fresh variable, and does so before applying
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
; this ensures that free variables that are already shared are not shared again. Because of this design, the translations preserve consistency.

Appendix to

C.1.
Encoding
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}

C.1.1.
Auxiliary Encoding: From
\lambda_{\oplus}
into
\widehat{\lambda}_{\oplus}

Proposition 39
.

The encoding commutes with linear substitution:
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N/x{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\{\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/x{|\!\}}

Proof C.1
.

By induction of the structure of
M
in
M{\{\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/x{|\!\}}
.

Proposition 40

(Well-typedness preservation for
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}-{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
)
.

Let
B
and
\mathbb{M}
be a bag and an expression in
\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, respectively.

(1)

If
\Gamma\vdash B:\sigma

then
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
.

(2)

If
\Gamma\vdash\mathbb{M}:\sigma

then
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
.

Proof C.2
.

By mutual induction on the typing derivations for
B
and
\mathbb{M}
, with an analysis of the last rule applied.

Part (1) includes two cases:

(1)

Rule
\mathtt{[T:\mathtt{1}]}
: Then
B=\mathtt{1}
and the thesis follows trivially,
because the encoding of terms/bags
(cf. Figure
8
)
ensures that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=\mathtt{1}
.

(2)

Rule
\mathtt{[T:bag]}
. Then
B=\Lbag M\Rbag\cdot A
, where
M
is a term and
A
is a bag,
and

\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma

\Delta\vdash A:\pi

\mathtt{[T:bag]}

\Gamma\wedge\Delta\vdash\Lbag M\Rbag\cdot A:\sigma\wedge\pi

By the IHs, we have both
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
and
\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\pi
. The thesis then follows by applying Rule
\mathtt{[TS:bag]}
in
\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\pi

\mathtt{[TS:bag]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\vdash\Lbag{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\Rbag\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma\wedge\pi

Part (2) considers six cases:

(1)

Rule
\mathtt{[T:var]}
: Then
\mathbb{M}=x

and

\mathtt{[T:var]}

x:\sigma\vdash x:\sigma

By the encoding of terms
(cf. Fig.
8

), we infer
x:\sigma\vdash x:\sigma
and so
the thesis holds immediately.

(2)

Rule
\mathtt{[T:abs]}
: Then
\mathbb{M}=\lambda x.M

and

\Gamma,x:\sigma^{n}\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[T:abs]}

\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.M:\sigma^{n}\rightarrow\tau

By the encoding of terms (cf. Fig.
8
), we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]
, where
\#(x,M)=n
and each
x_{i}
is fresh.

We work on the premise
\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{n}\vdash M:\tau
before appealing to the IH.

Then, by
n
applications of Lemma
22
to this judgment, we obtain

{\Gamma},{x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{n}:\sigma}\vdash{M}\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle:\tau

(5)

By IH on (
5
) we have

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},{x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{n}:\sigma}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

(6)

Starting from (
6
), we then have the following type derivation for
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
, which concludes the proof for this case:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{n}:\sigma\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\mathtt{[TS:share]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},x:\sigma^{n}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[TS:abs\text{-}sh]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash\lambda x.({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\leftarrow x]):\sigma^{n}\rightarrow\tau

(3)

Rule
\mathtt{[T:app]}
: Then
\mathbb{M}=M\ B
and

\Gamma\vdash M:\pi\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\vdash B:\pi

\mathtt{[T:app]}

\Gamma\wedge\Delta\vdash M\ B:\tau

By IH we have both

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\pi\rightarrow\tau

and

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\pi
,
and the thesis follows easily by Rule
\mathtt{[TS:app]}
in
\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\pi\rightarrow\tau

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\pi

\mathtt{[TS:app]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\ {\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

(4)

Rule
\mathtt{[T:ex\text{-}sub]}
: Then
\mathbb{M}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

and the proof is split in two cases, depending on the shape of
B
:

(a)

B=\mathtt{1}
. In this case,
\mathbb{M}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and we obtain the following type derivation:

\vdash\mathtt{1}:\omega

\Gamma\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[T:weak]}

\Gamma,x:\omega\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[T:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma\vdash M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

By IH we have both
\vdash\mathtt{1}:\omega
and
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau
.
By the encoding of terms (Figure
8
),
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, and the result holds
by the following type derivation:

\vdash\mathtt{1}:\omega

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\mathtt{[TS:weak]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},x:\omega\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[TS:ex\text{-}sub]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

(b)

B=\Lbag N_{1},\ldots,N_{n}\Rbag
,

Suppose w.l.o.g. that
