, then
B=\Lbag N_{1},N_{2}\Rbag
and

\Delta_{1}\vdash N_{1}:\sigma

\Delta_{2}\vdash N_{2}:\sigma

\mathtt{[T:bag]}

\Delta_{1}\wedge\Delta_{2}\vdash\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag\cdot\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag:\sigma^{2}

\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{2}\vdash M:\tau

\mathtt{[T:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma\wedge\Delta_{1}\wedge\Delta_{2}\vdash M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

By IH we have

\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma

and

\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
.
We can expand

\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{2}\vdash M:\tau

into

\Gamma,x:\sigma\wedge\sigma\vdash M:\tau
.
By Lemma
22
and the IH on this last sequent we obtain

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},y_{1}:\sigma,y_{2}:\sigma\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

where
\#(x,M)=2
and
y_{1},y_{2}
are fresh variables with the same type as
x
.

Now, by the encoding of terms (Figure
8
), we have

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1},N_{2}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\displaystyle\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}+

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\displaystyle\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{\prime}

We give typing derivations in
\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
for each summand. First, let
\Pi_{1}
be the following derivation:

\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma

\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},y_{1}:\sigma,y_{2}:\sigma\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},y_{2}:\sigma,\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

Similarly, we can obtain a derivation
\Pi_{2}
for:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

From
\Pi_{1}
,
\Pi_{2}
, and Rule
\mathtt{[TS:sum]}
, the thesis follows:

\Pi_{1}\qquad\Pi_{2}

\mathtt{[TS:sum]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\vdash\mathbb{M}^{\prime}:\tau

(5)

Rule
\mathtt{[T:weak]}
: Then
\mathbb{M}=M
and

\Gamma\vdash M:\sigma\qquad x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)

\mathtt{[T:weak]}

\Gamma,x:\omega\vdash M:\sigma

Because
\mathtt{[TS:weak]}
is a silent typing rule in
\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, we have that
x\not\in\mathsf{fv}(M)
and so this case does not apply.

(6)

Rule
\mathtt{[T:sum]}
:

This case follows easily by IH.

C.1.2.
Properties

We divide the proof of well-formedness preservation: we first prove it for
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}-{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
, then we extend it to
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}-{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
.

See
23

Proof C.3
.

By mutual induction on the typing derivations for
B
and
\mathbb{M}
, with an analysis of the last rule (from Fig.
4
) applied. We proceed with the following nine cases:

(1)

This case includes two subcases:

(a)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:wf\text{-}bag]}
.

Then by inversion of the typing derivation,

\Gamma\vdash B:\sigma

\mathtt{[F{:}wf-bag]}

\Gamma\models B:\sigma

By Propostion
40
we have
\Gamma\vdash B:\sigma
implies
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
. Notice that the encoding
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
given in
Fig.
8
, is a restriction of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
to
\lambda_{\oplus}
. Therefore,
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
, and the result follows after an application of
\mathtt{[FS{:}wf-bag]}
.

(b)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:bag]}
.

In this case
B=\Lbag M\Rbag\cdot A
, where
M
is a term and
A
is a bag, and we have the following derivation by inversion of the typing derivation:

\Gamma\models M:\sigma

\Delta\models A:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F:bag]}

\Gamma\wedge\Delta\models\Lbag M\Rbag\cdot A:\sigma^{k+1}

with
\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma)=\mathsf{fv}(M)
and
\mathtt{dom}(\Delta)=\mathsf{fv}(A)
. By the IHs, we have both

•

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
; and

•

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{k}
.

By applying Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}bag]}
from Fig.
7
, for
\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, we obtain the following derivation:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}bag]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models\Lbag{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\Rbag\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{k+1}

Since
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\Lbag M\Rbag\cdot A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=\Lbag{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\Rbag\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}},
one has
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\Lbag M\Rbag\cdot A{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{k+1}
, and the result follows.

(2)

This case is divided in seven subcases:

(a)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:wf\text{-}expr]}
.

Then the thesis follows trivially from type preservation in
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}-{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
of Proposition
40
.

(b)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:weak]}
.

In this case,
\mathbb{M}=M
and by inversion of the typing derivation we have the derivation

\Gamma_{1}\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[F:weak]}

\Gamma_{1},x:\omega\models M:\tau

Because
\mathtt{[weak]}
is a silent well-formed rule in
\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, we have that
x\not\in\mathsf{fv}(M)
and so this case does not apply.

(c)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:abs]}
.

In this case
\mathbb{M}=\lambda x.M
by inversion of the typing derivation
and we have the derivation:

\Gamma,{x}:\sigma^{n}\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[F:abs]}

\Gamma\models\lambda x.M:\sigma^{n}\rightarrow\tau

By the encoding given in Fig.
8
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\leftarrow x]
, where
\#(x,M)=n
and each
x_{i}
is fresh and has the same type as
x
.
From
{\Gamma},{x}:\sigma^{n}\models{M}:\tau
, we obtain after
n
applications of Proposition
21
and Lemma
22
:

{\Gamma},{x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{n}:\sigma}\models{M}\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle:\tau

By IH we have:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},{x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{n}:\sigma}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

which gives us the following derivation:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{n}:\sigma\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},x:\sigma^{n}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}abs\text{-}sh]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\models\lambda x.({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\leftarrow x]):\sigma^{n}\rightarrow\tau

and the result follows.

(d)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:app]}
.

In this case
\mathbb{M}=M\ B
, and by inversion of the typing derivation we have the derivation:

\Gamma\models M:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[F:app]}

\Gamma\wedge\Delta\models M\ B:\tau

By IH we have both

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

and

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{k}
,
and the result follows easily by Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}
in
\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
:

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{j}\rightarrow\tau

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{k}

\mathtt{[FS{:}app]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\ {\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

(e)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:ex\text{-}sub]}
.

Then
\mathbb{M}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

and the proof is split in two cases, depending on the shape of
B
:

(i)

When
\#(x,M)=\mathsf{size}(B)=k\geq 1
.

Then we have
B=\Lbag N_{1},\ldots,N_{n}\Rbag
,
n\geq 1
.
Suppose w.l.o.g. that
n=2
, then
B=\Lbag N_{1},N_{2}\Rbag
and by inversion of the typing derivation we have the following derivation:

\Delta_{1}\models N_{1}:\sigma

\Delta_{2}\models N_{2}:\sigma

\mathtt{[F:bag]}

\Delta_{1}\wedge\Delta_{2}\models\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag\cdot\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag:\sigma^{2}

\Gamma_{1},{x}:\sigma^{2}\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[F:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma_{1}\wedge\Delta_{1}\wedge\Delta_{2}\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Delta_{1},\Delta_{2}
.
By IH we have both

•

\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
; and

•

\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\vdash{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma
; and

•

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},{x}:\sigma^{2}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

We can expand

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},{x}:\sigma^{2}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau
into
\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},x:\sigma\wedge\sigma\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau
, which gives
\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},y_{1}:\sigma,y_{2}:\sigma\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle:\tau
, after two applications of Proposition
21
along with the application of Lemma
22
, with
y_{1},y_{2}
fresh variables of the same type as
x
. Since the encoding
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\cdot{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
commutes with the linear substitution
\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\cdot$}\!/\mbox{\small$\cdot$}\rangle
(Proposition
21
), if follows that,
\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},y_{1}:\sigma,y_{2}:\sigma\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau
.

Let
\Pi_{1}
be the derivation obtained after two consecutive applications of Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}lin\text{-}sub]}
:

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},y_{1}:\sigma,y_{2}:\sigma\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},y_{2}:\sigma,\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

Similarly, we can obtain a derivation
\Pi_{2}
for:

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}:\tau

By the encoding given in Figure
8
, we have

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1},N_{2}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\displaystyle\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}+

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}{\displaystyle\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$y_{1},y_{2}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}

Therefore,

\Pi_{1}

\Pi_{2}

\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta_{2}}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1},N_{2}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

and the result follows.

(ii)

\#(x,M)=k\neq\mathsf{size}(B)
.

In this case,
\mathsf{size}(B)=j
for some
j\neq k
, and by inversion of the typing derivation we have the following derivation:

\Delta\models B:\sigma^{j}

\Gamma_{1},{x}:\sigma^{k}\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[F:ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma_{1}\wedge\Delta\models M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}\wedge\Delta
. By IH we have both

•

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{j}
; and

•

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},\hat{x}:\sigma^{k}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau
.

We analyse two cases, depending on the number
k
of occurrences of
x
in
M
:

(A)

k=0
.

From
\Gamma_{1},{x:\omega}\models M:\tau
, which we get
\Gamma_{1}\models M:\tau
, via Rule
\mathtt{[F:weak]}
. The IH gives
\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau
, which entails:

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},x:\omega\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\leftarrow x]:\tau

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{j}

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

By the encoding given in Figure
8
,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, and the result follows.

(B)

k>0
.

By applying Proposition
21
in
\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},{x}:\sigma^{k}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau
, we obtain

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},x_{1}:\sigma,\ldots,x_{k}:\sigma\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle:\tau

From Proposition
21
and Lemma
22
, it follows that
\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},x_{1}:\sigma,\ldots,x_{k}:\sigma\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau
, which entails
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
since
k\neq 0
. First we give
\Pi
:

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger}\ ,x_{1}:\sigma,\ldots,x_{k}:\sigma\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\widehat{\Gamma_{1}}^{\dagger},x:\sigma^{k}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$x_{1}.\cdots,x_{k}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[x_{1}.\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

finally we give the full derivation:

\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\sigma^{j}

\Pi

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\dagger},\widehat{\Delta}^{\dagger}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle x_{1}.\cdots,x_{k}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[x_{1}.\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

(f)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:fail]}
.

The result follows trivially, because the encoding of failure in Fig.
8
is such that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}
.

(g)

Rule
\mathtt{[F:sum]}
.

This case follows easily by IH.

See
24

Proof C.4
.

By mutual induction on the typing derivations
\Gamma\models B:\sigma
and
\Gamma\models\mathbb{M}:\sigma
, exploiting both Proposition
21
and Lemma
22
. The analysis for bags (Part 1) follows directly from the IHs and will be omitted.

As for Part 2, there are two main cases to consider:

(1)

\mathbb{M}=M
.

Without loss of generality, assume
\mathsf{fv}(M)=\{x,y\}
. Then,

{x}:\sigma_{1}^{j},{y}:\sigma_{2}^{k}\models M:\tau

(7)

where
\#(x,M)=j
and

\#(y,M)=k
, for some positive integers
j
and
k
.

After
j+k
applications of Lemma
22

we obtain:

x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}\models M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle:\tau

where
\widetilde{x}=x_{1},\ldots,x_{j}

and
\widetilde{y}=y_{1},\ldots,y_{k}
.
From Proposition
21
and Lemma
22

one has

{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\ldots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\ldots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

Since
{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\ldots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\ldots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}}=x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\ldots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\ldots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}
,
we have the following derivation:

{x_{1}:\sigma_{1},\cdots,x_{j}:\sigma_{1},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

x:\sigma_{1}^{j},y_{1}:\sigma_{2},\cdots,y_{k}:\sigma_{2}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

x:\sigma_{1}^{j},y:\sigma_{2}^{k}\models{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x][\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]:\tau

By expanding Def.
3.4
, we have

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x][\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y],

which completes the proof for this case.

(2)

\mathbb{M}=M_{1}+\cdots+M_{n}
.

This case proceeds easily by IH, using Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
.

C.2.
Completeness and Soundness

See
25

Proof C.5
.

By induction on the rule from Fig.
2
applied to infer
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}
, distinguishing three cases. Below
\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}
abbreviates
[\widetilde{x_{1}}\leftarrow x_{1}]\ldots[\widetilde{x_{k}}\leftarrow x_{k}]
:

(1)

The rule applied is
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
.

In this case,

\mathbb{N}=(\lambda x.M)B
, the reduction is

\mathtt{[R:Beta]}

(\lambda x.M)B\longrightarrow M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

and
\mathbb{M}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\}
and
\widetilde{x_{i}}=x_{i_{1}},\ldots,x_{i_{j_{i}}}
, where
j_{i}=\#(x_{i},N)
, for
1\leq i\leq k
.
On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}(\lambda x.M)B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

(8)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}((\lambda x.M)B)\langle{\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}}\rangle\cdots\langle{\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}(\lambda x.M^{{}^{\prime}})B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\lambda x.M^{{}^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle=((\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime}}\langle{\widetilde{y}/x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]){\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime}}\langle{\widetilde{y}/x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}=\mathbb{L}

where we define
M^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
to be
M
and
B
after the substitutions of
\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots
\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle
. On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

(9)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle{\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}}\rangle\cdots\langle{\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

We need to analyse two sub-cases: either
\#(x,M^{\prime})=\mathsf{size}(B)=k\geq 1
or
\#(x,M^{\prime})=k
and our first sub-case is not met.

(1)

If
\#(x,M^{\prime})=\mathsf{size}(B)=k\geq 1
then we can reduce
\mathbb{L}
using Rule
\mathtt{[RS:Ex-sub]}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}\longrightarrow\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime}}\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{y}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(n)/y_{n}{|\!\rangle}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

From (
8
) and (
9
) and
\widetilde{y}=y_{1}\ldots y_{n}
, one has the desired result.

(2)

Otherwise,
\#(x,M)=k
(either
k=0
or
k\neq\mathsf{size}(B)
).

Expanding the encoding in (
9
) :

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime}}\langle{\widetilde{y}/x}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

Therefore
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\mathbb{L}
and
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
.

(2)

The rule applied is
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
.

Then
\mathbb{N}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is

\mathsf{head}(M)=x\quad B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{n}\Rbag,\ n\geq 1\quad\#(x,M)=n

\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}

M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{n}M{\{\!|}N_{i}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B\setminus\!\!\setminus N_{i}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

with
\mathbb{M}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}M{\{\!|}N_{i}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B\setminus\!\!\setminus N_{i}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\mathsf{fv}(M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N1\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{k}\}
. We distinguish two cases:

(1)

n=1
.

Then
B=\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag
and
\mathbb{N}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle=\mathbb{M}
.

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}(M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1}^{\prime}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle y_{1}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]},\text{ notice that }\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=y_{1}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{{}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Lin\text{-}Fetch]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\{\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

where we define
M^{\prime}
and
N_{1}^{\prime}
to be
M
and
N_{1}
after the substitutions of
\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots
\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle
; similarly, we define
M^{\prime\prime}
to be
M^{\prime}
after the substitution of
y_{1}
for
x
. On the other hand,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N_{1}^{\prime}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N_{1}^{\prime}/x{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

By the congruence defined in Fig.
13
for
\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
, one has
M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\equiv_{\lambda}M
.

Therefore,
\mathbb{M}=M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\equiv_{\lambda}M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}=\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
. Expanding
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{j}}/x_{j}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}{\{\!|}N_{1}^{\prime}/x{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\{\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/x{|\!\}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

Hence,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
and the result follows.

(2)

n>1

, we take

fresh variables.
On the one hand, we have:

(10)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle=({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle y_{1},y_{2}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{2}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}/y_{2}{|\!\rangle}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{2}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}+{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\{\!|}N_{2}/x{|\!\}}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\Lbag N_{1}\Rbag$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

The reductions in (

) and (
11
) lead to identical expressions, up to renaming of shared variables, which are taken to be fresh by definition. In both cases, we have taken the same fresh variables.

(3)

The rule applied is
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
and
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
. There are two possible cases. Below
\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}
abbreviates
[\widetilde{x_{1}}\leftarrow x_{1}]\cdots[\widetilde{x_{n}}\leftarrow x_{n}]
:

(I)

\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fail]}

Then
\mathbb{N}=M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is

\#(x,M)\neq\mathsf{size}(B)\qquad\widetilde{y}=(\mathsf{mfv}(M)\setminus x)\uplus\mathsf{mfv}(B)

\mathtt{[R:Fail]}

M\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}

where
\mathbb{M}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
. Below assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
.

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}\langle y_{1},\cdots,y_{k}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[y_{1},\cdots,y_{k}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail]}}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\mathbb{L}

where we define
M^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
to be
M
and
B
after the substitutions of
\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots
\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle
. On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\mathbb{L}

Therefore,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
and the result follows.

(II)

\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}
.

Then
\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ B
and the reduction is

\mathsf{size}(B)=k\qquad\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{mfv}(B)

\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ B\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\uplus\widetilde{z}}

and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\uplus\widetilde{z}}
. Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
.

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ B\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}\ B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\ {\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}\ {\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\cup\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\mathbb{L}

where we define
B^{\prime}
to be
B
after the substitutions of
\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle
. Similarly,
\widetilde{y^{\prime}}
and
\widetilde{z^{\prime}}
are
\widetilde{y}
and
\widetilde{z}
after the substitution
\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle
.
On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\uplus\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\cup\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\mathbb{L}

Therefore,
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, and the result follows.

(III)

\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}

Then
\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and the reduction is

\mathsf{size}(B)=k

\#(x,\widetilde{y})+k\not=0

\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{mfv}(B)

\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}

\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{z}}

and
\mathbb{M}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{z}}
.
Below we assume
\mathsf{fv}(\mathbb{N})=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}\}
.

On the one hand, we have: (below
\widetilde{y}=y_{1},\ldots,y_{m}
)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

(12)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}\langle y_{1}/x\rangle\cdots\langle y_{m}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime\prime}}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{2}]}}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\setminus x)\cup\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

As
\widetilde{y}
consists of free variables, in
\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle
the substitutions also occur on
\widetilde{y}
resulting in a new
\widetilde{y^{\prime}}
where all
x_{i}
’s are replaced with their fresh components in
\widetilde{x_{i}}
. Similarly for
z^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
as well as
\widetilde{y^{\prime\prime}}
being
\widetilde{y^{\prime}}
with each
x
replaced with a fresh
y_{i}
. On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

(13)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\setminus x)\uplus\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\mathtt{fail}^{(\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\setminus x)\cup\widetilde{z^{\prime}}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

The reductions in (
12
) and (
13
) lead to identical expressions.

As before, the reduction via Rule
\mathtt{[R]}
could occur inside a context (cf. Rules
\mathtt{[R:TCont]}
and
\mathtt{[R:ECont]}
). We consider only the case when the contextual rule used is
\mathtt{[R:TCont]}
. We have
\mathbb{N}=C[N]
. When we have
C[N]\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}C[M]
such that
N\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}M
we need to show that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}C[N]{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{j}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}C[M]{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
for some
j
dependent on
{\mathtt{[R]}}
.
Firstly, let us assume
{\mathtt{[R]}}=\mathtt{[R:Cons_{2}]}
then we take
j=1
. Let us take
C[\cdot]
to be
[\cdot]B
and
\mathsf{fv}(NB)=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}
then

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}NB{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}NB\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

We take
N^{\prime}B^{\prime}=NB\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle
, and by the IH that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
and hence we can deduce that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
where
M^{\prime}B^{\prime}=MB\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle
. Finally,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

and hence
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}C[N]{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}C[M]{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
.

See
26

Proof C.6
.

By induction on the structure of
\mathbb{N}
with the following six cases given below, where
\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}
abbreviates
[\widetilde{x_{1}}\leftarrow x_{1}]\ldots[\widetilde{x_{k}}\leftarrow x_{k}]
:

(1)

\mathbb{N}=x
:

Then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}x{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=x_{1}[x_{1}\leftarrow x]
, and no reductions can be performed.

(2)

\mathbb{N}=\lambda x.N
:

Suppose
\mathsf{fv}(N)=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}
. Then,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\lambda x.N{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\lambda x.N\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\lambda x.N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}=\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}\langle\widetilde{y}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]},

where
N^{\prime}
is
N
after the substitutions
\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle
and no reductions can be performed.

(3)

\mathbb{N}=NB
:

Suppose
\mathsf{fv}(NB)=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\}
. Then

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}NB{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}NB\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

(14)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

where
\widetilde{x_{i}}=x_{i1},\ldots,x_{ij_{i}}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
and
N^{\prime},B^{\prime}
are
N
and
B
after performing the substitutions
\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle
.
By the reduction rules in Fig.
5
there are three possible reductions starting in
\mathbb{N}
:

(a)

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}
reduces via rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}
.

In this case
N=\lambda x.N_{1}
, and the encoding in (
14
) gives
N^{\prime}=N\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle
, which implies
N^{\prime}=\lambda x.N_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}
and the following holds:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}(\lambda x.N^{\prime}_{1}){\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}

\displaystyle=(\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}_{1}\langle\widetilde{y}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x])=(\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{{}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x])

Thus, we have the following
\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}
reduction from (
14
):

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=(\lambda x.{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

(15)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{{}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\mathbb{L}

where
N^{\prime\prime}
is
N^{\prime}
after the substitutions
\langle\widetilde{y}/x\rangle
. Notice that the expression
\mathbb{N}
can perform the following
\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
-reduction:

\mathbb{N}=(\lambda x.N_{1})B\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R:Beta]}}N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

Assuming
\mathbb{N^{\prime}}=N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, there are two cases:

(i)

\#(x,M)=\mathsf{size}(B)=k\geq 1
.

On the one hand:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}\langle y_{1},\cdots,y_{k}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/y_{k}{|\!\rangle}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/y_{k}{|\!\rangle}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

where
N_{1}^{\prime\prime}
is
N_{1}^{\prime}
after the substitution
\langle\widetilde{y}/x\rangle
.

On the other hand, after an application of Rule
\mathtt{[RS:Ex-Sub]}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/y_{k}{|\!\rangle}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

and the result follows.

(ii)

Otherwise, either
\#(x,N_{1})=k=0
or
\#(x,N_{1})\neq\mathsf{size}(B)
.
In this case:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{n}}/x_{n}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N_{1}^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{{}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small${\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}=\mathbb{L}

From (
15
):
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
and the result follows.

(b)

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}
reduces via rule
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}
.

In this case we would have
N=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}
, and the encoding in (
14
) gives
N^{\prime}=N\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\ldots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{n}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{n}$}\rangle
, which implies
N^{\prime}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}
, we let
\mathsf{size}(B)=k
and the following:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

(16)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y^{\prime}}\uplus\widetilde{z}}\widetilde{[x_{1n}\leftarrow x_{1n}]},\text{ where }\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{fv}(B^{\prime})

The expression
\mathbb{N}
can perform the following
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Cons_{1}]}
-reduction:

\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}\ B\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}\uplus\widetilde{z}}\text{ where }\widetilde{z}=\mathsf{mfv}(B)

(17)

From (
16
) and (
17
), we infer that
\mathbb{L}={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{N^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
and so the result follows.

(c)

Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}
.

This case follows from the IH.

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\widetilde{x_{1}}/x_{1}\rangle\cdots\langle\widetilde{x_{k}}/x_{k}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

(18)

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B^{\prime}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

\displaystyle=\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N^{\prime}\langle y_{1},\cdots,y_{n}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(n)/y_{n}{|\!\rangle}\widetilde{[x_{1k}\leftarrow x_{1k}]}

We also have:

N^{\prime\prime}\longrightarrow N^{\prime\prime\prime}

(2)

C.3.
Success Sensitiveness

See
27

Proof C.7
.

By induction on the structure of
M
. We only need to consider terms of the following form.

(1)

When
M=\checkmark
the case is immediate.

(2)

When
M=NB
with
\mathsf{fv}(NB)=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}
and
\#(x_{i},M)=j_{i}
we have that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}NB{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}NB\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x_{1}}\leftarrow x_{1}]\cdots[\widetilde{x_{k}}\leftarrow x_{k}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}NB{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})

and
\mathsf{head}(NB)=\mathsf{head}(N)
, by the IH we have
\mathsf{head}(N)=\checkmark\iff\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})=\checkmark
.

(3)

When
M=N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
, we must have that
\#(x,M)=\mathsf{size}(B)
for the head of this term to be
\checkmark
. Let
\mathsf{fv}(N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\}
and
\#(x_{i},M)=j_{i}
. We have that:

\displaystyle\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{1}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{1}$}\rangle\cdots\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x_{k}}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x_{k}$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}[\widetilde{x_{1}}\leftarrow x_{1}]\cdots[\widetilde{x_{k}}\leftarrow x_{k}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}(\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N\langle x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N\langle x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{k}{|\!\rangle})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N\langle x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}/x\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})

and
\mathsf{head}(N\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\mathsf{head}(N)
, by the IH we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{head}(N)=\checkmark\iff\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}N{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})=\checkmark

See
28

Proof C.8
.

By induction on the structure of expressions
\lambda^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
and
\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
. We proceed with the proof in two parts.

(1)

Suppose that
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

By operational completeness (Theorem
25
) we have that if
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}\mathbb{M^{\prime}}
then

(1)

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{\leq 2}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
;

(2)

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{+}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, for some
\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\equiv_{\lambda}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
.

(3)

If
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
and
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Fetch]}
then
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
;

Notice that neither our reduction rules (in Figure
5
), or our congruence
\equiv_{\lambda}
(in Figure
18
), or our encoding (
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\checkmark{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\checkmark
) create or destroy a
\checkmark
occurring in the head of term. By Proposition
27
the encoding preserves the head of a term being
\checkmark
. The encoding acts homomorphically over sums, therefore, if a
\checkmark
appears as the head of a term in a sum, it will stay in the encoded sum. We can iterate the operational completeness lemma and obtain the result.

(2)

Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

From Def.
5.2.3
we have that
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}\implies\exists M_{1},\cdots,M_{k}.~\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow^{*}M_{1}+\cdots+M_{k}\text{ and }\mathsf{head}(M_{j})=\checkmark,

for some
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
.

Notice that if
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
is itself a term headed with
\checkmark
, say
\mathsf{head}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}})=\checkmark
, then
\mathbb{M}
is itself headed with
\checkmark
, from Proposition
27
.

Based on the shape of
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, we consider two cases.
The first case, when
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=M_{1}+\ldots+M_{k}
,
k\geq 2
, and
\checkmark
occurs in the head of an
M_{j}
, follows a similar reasoning. Then
\mathbb{M}
has one of the forms:

(1)

\mathbb{M}=N_{1}
, then
N_{1}
must contain the subterm
M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and
\mathsf{size}(B)=\#(x,M)
.
Since,

{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}=\displaystyle\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}B{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}})}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\widetilde{x}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\bullet}}{\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{i}{|\!\rangle}\ldots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(k)/x_{i}{|\!\rangle}
, we can apply Proposition
27
as we may apply
\mathsf{head}_{\sum}({\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}M\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}})
.

(2)

\mathbb{M}=N_{1}+\ldots+N_{l}
for
l\geq 2
.

The reasoning is similar and uses the fact that the encoding distributes homomorphically over sums.

The second case is when
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow^{*}M_{1}+\ldots+M_{k}
, and
\mathsf{head}(M_{j})=\checkmark
, for some
j
and
M_{j}
. By operational soundness (Theorem
26
) we have that if
{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}
then there exist
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow_{\mathtt{[R]}}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
and

(1)

If
\mathtt{[R]}=\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
\mathbb{L}\longrightarrow^{\leq 1}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
;

(2)

If
\mathtt{[R]}\neq\mathtt{[R:Beta]}
then
\mathbb{L}\longrightarrow^{*}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\llparenthesis}\mathbb{M}^{{}^{\prime\prime}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.5,0}\rrparenthesis^{\circ}}
, for
\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\equiv_{\lambda}\mathbb{M}^{\prime\prime}
.

The reasoning is similar to the previous case, since our reduction rules do not introduce/eliminate
\checkmark
occurring in the head of terms and by taking
\mathbb{L}
to be
M_{1}+\ldots+M_{k}
with
\mathsf{head}(M_{j})=\checkmark
, for some
j
and
M_{j}
the result follows.

By mutual induction on the typing derivation of

(1)

\displaystyle(x_{i}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(M)};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{i}}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}A{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid y_{i}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})

(duality) and

, we infer:

\displaystyle\oplus(({\binampersand}\mathbf{1})\mathbin{\bindnasrepma}(\oplus{\binampersand}((\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}})\otimes({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma\wedge\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,j)}))))

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma\wedge\sigma\wedge\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,j)}

Therefore,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Lbag M\Rbag\cdot A{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger},\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma\wedge\sigma\wedge\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,j)}

(2)

The proof of type preservation for expressions, relies on the analysis of nine cases:

(a)

Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}wf\text{-}expr]}
:

Then we have the following derivation:

\Gamma^{\dagger}\vdash\mathbb{M}:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}wf\text{-}expr]}

\Gamma^{\dagger}\models\mathbb{M}:\tau

Cases follow from their corresponding case from
\mathtt{[FS{:}\text{-}]}
. In the case of
\mathtt{[TS{:}var]}
we have:

\mathtt{[TS{:}var]}

x:\tau\vdash x:\tau

By Definition
5.3.3
,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}x:\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}=x:{\binampersand}\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}}
, and by Figure
16
,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}x{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};[x\leftrightarrow u]
. The thesis holds thanks to the following derivation:

\mathtt{[(Tid)]}

[x\leftrightarrow u]\vdash x:\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

\mathtt{[T{\binampersand}^{x}_{d})]}

x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};[x\leftrightarrow u]\vdash x:{\binampersand}\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

(b)

Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}abs\text{-}sh]}
:

Then
\mathbb{M}=\lambda x.(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])
, and the derivation is:

\Delta^{\dagger},x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{k}:\sigma\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Delta^{\dagger},x:\sigma\wedge\cdots\wedge\sigma\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau\quad x\notin\Delta^{\dagger}

\mathtt{[FS{:}abs\text{-}sh]}

\Delta^{\dagger}\models\lambda x.(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]):\sigma^{k}\rightarrow\tau

To simplify the proof we will consider
k=2
(
k>2
follows similarly).

By the IH, we have

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger},x_{1}:\sigma,x_{2}:\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}.

From
Def.
5.3.2
and Def.
5.3.3
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger},x_{1}:\sigma,x_{2}:\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x_{1}:{\binampersand}\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}},x_{2}:{\binampersand}\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\lambda x.M[x_{1},x_{2}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=u.\overline{\mathtt{some}};u(x).{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1},x_{2}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=u.\overline{\mathtt{some}};u(x).x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}

\displaystyle\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{1},x_{2})};x(x_{1}).{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\displaystyle\overline{x}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{2}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{2})};}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{1,0,0}{\displaystyle x(x_{2}).}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(y_{3}).(y_{3}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{3}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}}

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\displaystyle\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})))}

We shall split the expression into three parts:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}N_{1}}

\displaystyle=x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(y_{3}).(y_{3}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{3}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})
