\displaystyle=x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{2}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{2})};

\displaystyle x(x_{2}).N_{1})

\displaystyle N_{3}

\displaystyle=u.\overline{\mathtt{some}};u(x).x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};

\displaystyle x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{1},x_{2})};x(x_{1}).N_{2})

and we obtain the derivation for term

as follows:

\mathtt{[T\bot]}

\mathtt{[T\oplus^{x}_{\widetilde{w}}]}

\mathtt{[T{\binampersand}^{x}]}

\mathtt{[T\otimes]}

\mathtt{[T{\binampersand}_{d}^{x}]}

.
In order to obtain a type derivation for

, consider the derivation

\mathtt{[T\mathbin{\bindnasrepma}]}

.

We can then conclude that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}MB{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}
:

For each
B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)\qquad\nabla_{i}

\mathtt{[T{\binampersand}]}

\displaystyle{\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}))}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

and the result follows.

(d)

Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}
:

Then
\mathbb{M}=M[x_{1},\dots x_{k}\leftarrow x]
and

\Delta^{\dagger},x_{1}:\sigma,\cdots,x_{k}:\sigma\models M:\tau\quad x\notin\Delta^{\dagger}\quad k\not=0

\mathtt{[FS{:}share]}

\Delta^{\dagger},x:\sigma_{k}\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

The proof for this case is contained within 2(b).

(e)

Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}
:

Then
\mathbb{M}=M[\leftarrow x]
and

\Gamma^{\dagger}\models M:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}weak]}

\Gamma^{\dagger},x:\omega\models M[\leftarrow x]:\tau

However
\Gamma^{\dagger},x:\omega
is not a core context hence we disallow the case.

(f)

Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}
:

Then
\mathbb{M}=M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
and

\Delta^{\dagger}\models B:\sigma^{j}

\Gamma^{\dagger},x:\sigma^{k}\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]:\tau

\mathtt{[FS{:}ex\text{-}sub]}

\Gamma^{\dagger},\Delta^{\dagger}\models M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle:\tau

By Proposition
30
and IH we have both

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x:\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,n)}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}

\displaystyle\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,m)}

From Def.
5.3.2
, we have

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

Therefore, for each
B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)
, we obtain the following derivation
\Pi_{i}
:

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x:\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,n)}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,m)}

\mathtt{[Tcut]}

(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

We must have that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma^{j}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,m)}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma^{k}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{(\tau,n)}
which holds by the conditions in Proposition
30
.
Therefore, from
\Pi_{i}
and multiple applications of
\mathtt{[T{\binampersand}]}
it follows that

\forall\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\hskip 28.45274pt\Pi_{i}

\mathtt{[T{\binampersand}]}

\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{w};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

that is,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1},x_{2}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger},\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}
and the result follows.

From Def.
5.3.2
,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N)};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})
and

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x:{\binampersand}\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}}

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

\mathtt{[T\oplus^{x}]}

x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N)};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},x:\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

\mathtt{[TCut]}

(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N)};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}

Observe that for the application of Rule
\mathtt{[TCut]}
we used the fact that
\overline{\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}}={\binampersand}\overline{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}}
. Therefore,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\vdash{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Gamma^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\Delta^{\dagger}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}},u:{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\tau{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}
and the result follows.

\mathtt{[FS{:}fail]}
:

Then
\mathbb{M}=M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid x_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\cdots\mid x_{k}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}

(i)

Rule
\mathtt{[FS{:}sum]}
:

This case follows easily by IH.

D.2.
Completeness and Soundness

See
32

Proof D.3
.

By induction on the structure of
\mathbb{M}
. Let us consider first two conditions 1 and 2 as other conditions are analogous. The congruence rules that concern the sharing construct of condition 1 are:

\begin{array}[]{rll}\!\!\!M[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle&\equiv_{\lambda}M&\\
\!\!\!MA[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle&\equiv_{\lambda}(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)A&\!\text{with }x_{i}\in\widetilde{x}\Rightarrow x_{i}\not\in\mathsf{fv}(A)\\
\!\!\!M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$A$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle&\equiv_{\lambda}(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle)[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$A$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle&\!\text{with }x_{i}\in\widetilde{x}\Rightarrow x_{i}\not\in\mathsf{fv}(A)\\
\end{array}

Notice that these rules neither add or remove occurrences of shared variables neither do they allow shared variables to be extruded from their bindings by their side conditions. Also, they do not introduce new sharing on already shared variables.
Hence, conditions 1(i) to 1(iv) are preserved by these rules.

Now consider the congruence rules concerning the explicit substitution of condition 2:

\begin{array}[]{rll}MB{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}&\equiv_{\lambda}(M{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle})B&\text{with }x\not\in\mathsf{fv}(B)\\
M{\langle\!|}N_{2}/y{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\rangle}&\equiv_{\lambda}M{\langle\!|}N_{1}/x{|\!\rangle}{\langle\!|}N_{2}/y{|\!\rangle}&\text{with }x\not\in\mathsf{fv}(N_{2}),y\notin\mathsf{fv}(N_{1})\\
\end{array}

As before, variables are not duplicated or eliminated from terms and by the side conditions of the rules they cannot extrude bound variables. Similarly, the rules do not introduce any sharing or new free variables.
Hence conditions 2(i) to 2(iv) are satisfied.

See
33

Proof D.4
.

Let us consider each part:

(1)

We proceed by induction on the structure of
N
.

(I)

N=x
.

Then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}x{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
. Hence
I=\emptyset
and
\widetilde{y}=\emptyset
.

(II)

N=(M\ B)
.

Then
\mathsf{head}(M\ B)=\mathsf{head}(M)=x
and

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M\ B{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}))

and the result follows by induction on
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
.

(III)

N=M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]
. Not possible due to the assumption of partially open terms.

(IV)

N=(M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

Then
\mathsf{head}((M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle)=\mathsf{head}((M[\widetilde{y}\leftarrow y]))=x
when
\widetilde{y}=\emptyset,\ B=\mathtt{1}
and
\mathsf{head}(M)=x
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(M[\leftarrow y])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=(\nu y)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow y]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{y})

\displaystyle=(\nu y)(y.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{y}(z).(z.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};z.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid y.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mid

\displaystyle y.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y(z).(z.\overline{\mathtt{some}};z.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid y.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

Then the result follows by induction on
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
.

(V)

When
N=M{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}
, then
\mathsf{head}(M{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/y{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M)=x
and

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=(\nu y)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N^{\prime})};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

Then true by induction on
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

(2)

In this case, notice how reductions are only introduced when
N
has sub-term
(M[\leftarrow y])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$\mathtt{1}$}\!/\mbox{\small$y$}\rangle\!\rangle
from case 1(IV), however from the congruence of Fig.
18
we may rewrite this sub-term to be
M
which eliminates the need for reductions. Inductively, performing this application of
\equiv_{\lambda}
provides the result.

(3)

This case is similar to the first, with the clear difference that linear head substitution must also be used. However, we can inductively push the linear head substitution inside the term to reach the head variable. Consider the base case when
N=x
and we have some well-formed partially open term
M
. Then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\{\!|}M/x{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}x{\{\!|}M/x{|\!\}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
. Hence
I=\emptyset
and
\widetilde{y}=\emptyset
matching that of case 1(i).

Next, let us consider the case of
N{\{\!|}M/x{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}{\{\!|}M/x{|\!\}}=M^{\prime}{\{\!|}M/x{|\!\}}{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/y{|\!\rangle}
. By considering 1(V) we can see the evaluating the translation of creates the same process shape up to linear head substitution. Other cases follow analogously.

(4)

This is a consequence of both (2) and (3).

{nota}

We use the notation
\mathsf{fv}(M).\overline{\mathtt{none}}
and
\widetilde{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}
where
\mathsf{fv}(M)
or
\widetilde{x}
are equal to
x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}
to describe a process of the form
x_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\cdots\mid x_{k}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}

See
34

Proof D.5
.

By induction on the reduction rule applied to infer
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}
.
We have five cases.

(1)

Case
\mathtt{[RS{:}Beta]}
:

Then
\mathbb{N}=(\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B\longrightarrow M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle=\mathbb{M}

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\lambda x.M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)(v(x).{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

Therefore, by (

) and (

(2)

Case
\mathtt{[RS{:}Ex\text{-}Sub]}

We detail the encodings of

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

(33)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{1})};

\displaystyle x(x_{1}).x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{2}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mid

\displaystyle x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};x(y_{1}).x.\mathtt{some}_{y_{1},\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(x_{1}).(x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\displaystyle\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}\mid y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x(y_{2}).(y_{2}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{2}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x,y_{1},x_{1},y_{2})(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid y_{2}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{2}.\mathtt{close};

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\displaystyle\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid y_{2}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{2}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}})

On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x_{1})({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}})

(5)

Case
\mathtt{[RS{:}Fail]}
:

Then,

\mathbb{N}=M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle

Let us assume that

and we proceed similarly for

. Hence

for some

. On the one hand, we have (
38
), this can be seen in Fig.

.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

(38)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{1},\cdots,x_{k})};

\displaystyle x(x_{1}).\cdots x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{k}).(y_{k}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{k}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{k})};

\displaystyle x(x_{k}).x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(y_{k+1}).(y_{k+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{k+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\cdots)

\displaystyle\mid y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\cdots x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};x(y_{l}).x.\mathtt{some}_{y_{l},\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(x_{l}).(x_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};

\displaystyle x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{l+1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{l+1},\cdots,x_{k})};

\displaystyle x(x_{l+1}).\cdots x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{k}).(y_{k}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{k}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{k})};x(x_{k}).

\displaystyle x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(y_{k+1}).(y_{k+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{k+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\cdots)\mid

\displaystyle x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}\mid\cdots\mid x_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{l}}\mid y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\cdots\mid y_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}

\displaystyle x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x,x_{1},\cdots,x_{l})(x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{l+1},\cdots,x_{k})};x(x_{l+1}).\cdots

\displaystyle x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{k}).(y_{k}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{k}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{k})};x(x_{k}).

\displaystyle x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(y_{k+1}).(y_{k+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{k+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\mid

\displaystyle x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}\mid\cdots\mid x_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{l}}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x_{1},\cdots,x_{l})(u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid x_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\cdots\mid x_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}).\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid

\displaystyle x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}\mid\cdots\mid x_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{l}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\})\cup\mathsf{fv}(B).\overline{\mathtt{none}}

Figure 20
.
Reductions of an encoded explicit substitution

On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

(39)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\})\cup\mathsf{fv}(B).\overline{\mathtt{none}}

Therefore, by (
38
)
and (
39
) the result follows.

(6)

Case
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{1}]}
:

Then,

\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B
with
B=\Lbag N_{1},\dots,N_{k}\Rbag
and
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}=\mathbb{M}
, where
\widetilde{y}=\mathsf{fv}(B)
.

On the one hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}\ B{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

(40)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)(v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{y}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{y}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}

On the other hand, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=\bigoplus_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{x}\cup\widetilde{y}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{x}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{y}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}

Therefore, by (
40
)
and (

) the result follows.

(7)

Cases
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{2}]}
and
\mathtt{[RS{:}Cons_{3}]}
: These cases follow by IH similarly to Case 7.

See
35

Proof D.6
.

By induction on the structure of
\mathbb{N}
and then induction on the number of reductions of
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{*}Q

(1)

\mathbb{N}=x
,
\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\emptyset}
and
\mathbb{N}=\lambda x.(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])
.

These cases are trivial since no reduction can take place.

(2)

\mathbb{N}=(M\ B)
.

Then,

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(M\ B){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}))

and we are able to perform the reductions from
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(M\ B){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
.

We now proceed by induction on
k
, with
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{k}Q
.

The interesting case is when
k\geq 1
(the case
k=0
is trivial).

Then, for some process
R
and
n,m
such that
k=n+m
, we have the following:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{m}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)(R\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{n}Q

Thus, the first
m\geq 0
reduction steps are internal to
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}
; type preservation in
\mathsf{s}\pi
ensures that, if they occur, these reductions do not discard the possibility of synchronizing with
v.\mathtt{some}
. Then, the first of the
n\geq 0
reduction steps towards
Q
is a synchronization between
R
and
v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)}
.

We consider two sub-cases, depending on the values of
m
and
n
:

(I)

When
m=0
and
n\geq 1
:

Thus
R={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}
, and there are two possibilities of having an unguarded
v.\overline{\mathtt{some}}
or
v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}
without internal reductions. By the diamond property (Proposition
9
) we will be reducing each non-deterministic choice of a process simultaneously.
Then we have the following for each case:

(A)

M=(\lambda x.M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\qquad(p\geq 0)
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(\lambda x.M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}

\displaystyle=(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(\lambda x.M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N_{1})};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{y_{1}}\mid\cdots\mid y_{p}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N_{p})};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N_{p}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{y_{p}})

\displaystyle=(\nu\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(\lambda x.M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid Q^{\prime\prime}),\text{ for }\widetilde{y}=y_{1},\cdots,y_{p}

\displaystyle=(\nu\widetilde{y})(v.\overline{\mathtt{some}};v(x).{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid Q^{\prime\prime})

where

Q^{\prime\prime}=y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N_{1})};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{y_{1}}\mid\cdots\mid y_{p}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N_{p})};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N_{p}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{y_{p}}
.

With this shape for
M
, the encoding of
\mathbb{N}
becomes:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(M\ B){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)((\nu\widetilde{y})(v.\overline{\mathtt{some}};v(x).{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid Q^{\prime\prime})\mid

\displaystyle v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v,\widetilde{y})(v(x).{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u])\mid Q^{\prime\prime})=Q_{1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v,\widetilde{y},x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]\mid Q^{\prime\prime})=Q_{2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x,\widetilde{y})({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid Q^{\prime\prime})=Q_{3}

We also have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle=(\lambda x.M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}\ B

\displaystyle\equiv_{\lambda}((\lambda x.M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])B){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}=\mathbb{M}

Furthermore, we have:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid Q^{\prime\prime})

We consider different possibilities for
n\geq 1
; in all of the thesis holds:

(i)

n=1
:

Then
Q=Q_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{1}Q_{1}
. In addition,

(a)

Q_{1}\longrightarrow^{2}Q_{3}=Q^{\prime}
,

(b)

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
,

(c)

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q_{3}
.

and the result follows.

(ii)

n=2
:

Then
Q=Q_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{2}Q_{2}
. In addition,

•

Q_{2}\longrightarrow^{1}Q_{3}=Q^{\prime}
,

•

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}

•

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q_{3}

and the result follows.

(iii)

n\geq 3
:

Then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{3}Q_{3}\longrightarrow^{l}Q
, for
l\geq 0
. In addition,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
and
Q_{3}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
. By the IH, there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that
Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
. Finally,

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{3}Q_{3}\longrightarrow^{l}Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{M}^{\prime}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
, and the result follows.

(B)

M=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}=v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}

With this shape for
M
, the encoding of
\mathbb{N}
becomes:

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(M\ B){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)(v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\mathsf{fv}(B).\overline{\mathtt{none}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\mathsf{fv}(B).\overline{\mathtt{none}}

Also,

\mathbb{N}=\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}\ B\longrightarrow\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}\cup\mathsf{fv}(B)}=\mathbb{M}.

Furthermore,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sum_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}\cup\mathsf{fv}(B)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}\cup\mathsf{fv}(B)}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{\mathsf{PER}(B)}u.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\widetilde{z}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\mathsf{fv}(B).\overline{\mathtt{none}}

(II)

When
m\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
, the distinguish two cases:

(A)

n=0
:

Then,

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)(R\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))=Q,

\displaystyle\text{ and }{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{m}R.

By the IH there exist
R^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
such that
R\longrightarrow^{i}R^{\prime}
,
M\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=R^{\prime}
. Hence,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{m}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)(R\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))=Q

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{i}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)(R^{\prime}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}\mid[v\leftrightarrow u]))=Q^{\prime}

and so the
\widehat{\lambda}^{\lightning}_{\oplus}
term can reduce as follows:
\mathbb{N}=(M\ B)\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}M^{\prime}\ B=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

(B)

n\geq 1
:

Then
R
has an occurrence of an unguarded
v.\overline{\mathtt{some}}
or
v.\overline{\mathtt{none}}
, which implies it is of the form

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(\lambda x.M^{\prime}[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]){\langle\!|}N_{1}/y_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}N_{p}/y_{p}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}
or
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathtt{fail}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}
, and the case follows by IH.

This concludes the analysis for the case
\mathbb{N}=(M\,B)
.

(3)

\mathbb{N}=M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]
.

The sharing variable
x
is not free and the result follows by vacuity.

(4)

\mathbb{N}=(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
. Then,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

(I)

\mathsf{size}(\widetilde{x})=\mathsf{size}(B)
.

Then let us consider the shape of the bag
B
.

(A)

When
B=1

We have the following

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}1{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

\displaystyle=(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{i}).(y_{i}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{i}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mid

\displaystyle x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x(y_{n}).(y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x)(\overline{x}(y_{i}).(y_{i}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{i}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mid

\displaystyle x(y_{n}).(y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\qquad=Q_{1}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x,y_{i})(y_{i}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{i}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};

\displaystyle y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\qquad=Q_{2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x,y_{i})(y_{i}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid y_{n}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\qquad=Q_{3}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\qquad=Q_{4}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\qquad=Q_{5}

Notice how
Q_{2}
has a choice however the
x
name can be closed at any time so for simplicity we only perform communication across this name once all other names have completed their reductions.

Now proceed by induction on the number of reductions
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{k}Q
.

(i)

k=0
:

This case is trivial.

(ii)

k=1
: (
2\leq k\leq 4
: is similar.)

Then,
Q=Q_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{1}Q_{1}
. In addition,
Q_{1}\longrightarrow^{4}Q_{5}=Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{0}M[\leftarrow x]\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$1$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\equiv_{\lambda}M
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q_{5}
, and the result follows.

(iii)

k\geq 5
:

Then
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{5}Q_{5}\longrightarrow^{l}Q
, for
l\geq 0
. Since
Q_{5}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
, by the IH it follows that there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that
Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime},M\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.

Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{5}Q_{5}\longrightarrow^{l}Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
and by the contextual reduction one has
\mathbb{N}=(M[\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$1$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and the case holds.

(B)

B=\Lbag N_{1},\cdots,N_{l}\Rbag
, for
l\geq 1
.

Then, consider the reductions in Fig.
21
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{1},\cdots,x_{l})};

\displaystyle x(x_{1}).\cdots x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{l}).(y_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{l}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{l})};x(x_{l}).

\displaystyle x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{l+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))\cdots)\mid

\displaystyle x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B)};x(y_{1}).x.\mathtt{some}_{y_{1},\mathsf{fv}(B)};x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(x_{1}).(x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}

\displaystyle\mid y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\cdots x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};x(y_{l}).x.\mathtt{some}_{y_{l},\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(x_{l}).(x_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\displaystyle\llbracket}B_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{l}}\mid y_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{5l}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x,x_{1},y_{1},\cdots,x_{l},y_{1})(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid\cdots y_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{l}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid

\displaystyle x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};\overline{x}(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(M)};y_{l+1}.\mathtt{close};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{none}})\mid

\displaystyle x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}\mid y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\cdots x_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{l}}\mid y_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid

\displaystyle x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x(y_{l+1}).(y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{some}};y_{l+1}.\overline{\mathtt{close}}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};x.\overline{\mathtt{none}}))

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{5}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x_{1},y_{1},\cdots,x_{l},y_{1})(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid\cdots y_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{l}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}

\displaystyle\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}\mid y_{1}.\overline{\mathtt{none}}\mid\cdots x_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{l}}\mid y_{l}.\overline{\mathtt{none}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{l}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x_{1},\cdots,x_{l})({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}\mid\cdots\mid x_{l}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(l))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(l){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{l}})

\displaystyle=Q_{6l+5}

Figure 21
.
Reductions of encoded explicit substitution

The proof follows by induction on the number of reductions
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{k}Q
.

(i)

k=0
:

This case is trivial. Take
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q=Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
.

(ii)

1\leq k\leq 6l+5
:

Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{k}Q_{k}
.
Observing the reductions in Fig.
21
, one has

Q_{k}\longrightarrow^{6l+5-k}Q_{6l+5}=Q^{\prime}
,

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M\ {\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(l)/x_{l}{|\!\rangle}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M\ {\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(l)/x_{l}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q_{6l+5}
, and the result follows.

(iii)

k>6l+5
:

Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{6l+5}Q_{6l+5}\longrightarrow^{n}Q
, for
n\geq 1
. In addition,

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M\ {\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(l)/x_{l}{|\!\rangle}
and

Q_{6l+5}={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M\ {\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(l)/x_{l}{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
. By the IH there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that
Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
,

\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M\ {\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(l)/x_{l}{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}

and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
. Finally,

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{6l+5}Q_{6l+5}\longrightarrow^{n}Q\longrightarrow^{i}Q^{\prime}
and

\mathbb{N}\rightarrow\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}M\ {\langle\!|}B_{i}(1)/x_{1}{|\!\rangle}\cdots{\langle\!|}B_{i}(l)/x_{l}{|\!\rangle}\longrightarrow_{\equiv_{\lambda}}^{j}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
.

(II)

\mathsf{size}(\widetilde{x})>\mathsf{size}(B)
.

Then,

\mathbb{N}=M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
with
B=\Lbag N_{1},\cdots,N_{l}\Rbag,
for
k>l
. Also,

\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}=\mathbb{M}\ \text{ and }\ \widetilde{z}=(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\})\cup\mathsf{fv}(B).

On the one hand, we have Fig.
22
.
Hence
k=l+m
for some
m\geq 1

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]\ \langle\!\langle\raisebox{1.93748pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M[x_{1},\cdots,x_{k}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)(x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{1}).(y_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{1}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{1},\cdots,x_{k})};

\displaystyle x(x_{1}).\cdots x.\overline{\mathtt{some}}.\overline{x}(y_{k}).(y_{k}.\mathtt{some}_{\emptyset};y_{k}.\mathtt{close};{\bf 0}\mid x.\overline{\mathtt{some}};x.\mathtt{some}_{u,(\mathsf{fv}(M)\setminus x_{k})};x(x_{k}).

\displaystyle=Q_{7l+6}

Figure 22
.
Reductions of an encoded explicit substitution that leads to failure

Now we proceed by induction on the number of reductions
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{j}Q
.

(A)

j=0
:

This case is trivial.

(B)

1\leq j\leq 7l+6
:

Then,

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{j}Q_{j}\longrightarrow^{7l+6-j}Q_{7l+6}=Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}=\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q_{7l+6}
, and the result follows.

(C)

j>7l+6
:

Then,
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{7l+6}Q_{7l+6}\longrightarrow^{n}Q
, for
n\geq 1
. Also,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{1}\sum_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}\mathtt{fail}^{\widetilde{z}}
. However no further reductions can be performed.

(III)

\mathsf{size}(\widetilde{x})<\mathsf{size}(B)
.

Proceeds similarly to the previous case.

(5)

\mathbb{N}=M{\langle\!|}N^{\prime}/x{|\!\rangle}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

D.3.
Success Sensitiveness

See
36

Proof D.7
.

In both cases, by induction on the structure of
M
.

(1)

We only need to consider terms of the following form:

•

M=\checkmark
.

This case is immediate.

•

.

By definition,
\mathsf{head}(N\ B)=\mathsf{head}(N)
. Hence we consider that
\mathsf{head}(N)=\checkmark
. Then,

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N\ B{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}))

and by the IH
\checkmark
is unguarded in
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
after a sequence of reductions.

•

and

.

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow^{*}\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu\widetilde{x})({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x_{1}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(1))};

\displaystyle\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}{\displaystyle\llbracket}B_{i}(1){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{1}}\mid\cdots\mid x_{k}.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(B_{i}(k))};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}(k){\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x_{k}})

and by the IH
\checkmark
is unguarded in
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
after a sequence of reductions.

•

M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
.

By definition,
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})\checkmark
. Then,

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N)};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

and by the IH
\checkmark
is unguarded in
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
.

(2)

We only need to consider terms of the following form:

•

M=\checkmark
.

This case is trivial.

•

M=N\ B
.

Then,

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N\ B{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu v)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}\mid v.\mathtt{some}_{u,\mathsf{fv}(B)};\overline{v}(x).([v\leftrightarrow u]\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})).

The only occurrence of an unguarded
\checkmark
is within
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{v}
. By the IH we have that
\mathsf{head}(N)=\checkmark
and finally
\mathsf{head}(N\ B)=\mathsf{head}(N)
.

•

M=(N[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle
.

Then,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}(N[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x])\langle\!\langle\raisebox{2.15277pt}{\small$B$}\!/\mbox{\small$x$}\rangle\!\rangle{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{B_{i}\in\mathsf{PER}(B)}(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x})

However in both
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N[\widetilde{x}\leftarrow x]{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
and
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}B_{i}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}
we have that both are guarded and hence
\checkmark
cannot occur without synchronizations.

•

M=M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}
.

Then,

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=(\nu x)({\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\mid x.\mathtt{some}_{\mathsf{fv}(N)};{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}N{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{x}),

an unguarded occurrence of
\checkmark
can only occur within
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
. By the IH we have
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})=\checkmark
and hence
\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime}{\langle\!|}N/x{|\!\rangle})=\mathsf{head}(M^{\prime})
.

See
37

Proof D.8
.

We proceed with the proof in two parts.

(1)

Suppose that
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

By Def.
5.2.3
, there exists
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}=M_{1}+\cdots+M_{k}
such that
\mathbb{M}\longrightarrow^{*}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
and with

\mathsf{head}(M_{j})=\checkmark
, for some
j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
. By completeness there exists
Q
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{*}Q={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
.

We wish to show that there exists
Q^{\prime}
such that
Q\longrightarrow^{*}Q^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}
has an unguarded occurrence of
\checkmark
.

Since
Q={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
and due to compositionality and the homormorphic preservation of non-determinism, we have that

\displaystyle Q

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M_{1}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\oplus\cdots\oplus{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M_{k}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}

By Proposition
36
(1) we have that

\mathsf{head}(M_{j})=\checkmark\implies{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}M_{j}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{*}(P\mid\checkmark)\oplus Q^{\prime\prime}

for some
Q^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence,
Q\longrightarrow^{*}(P\mid\checkmark)\oplus Q^{\prime\prime}=Q^{\prime}
, as wanted.

(2)

Suppose that
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
. We will prove that
\mathbb{M}\Downarrow_{\checkmark}
.

By operational soundness (Theorem
35
): if
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{*}Q

then there exist
Q^{\prime}
and
\mathbb{N}^{\prime}
such that

Q\longrightarrow^{*}Q^{\prime}
,
\mathbb{N}\longrightarrow^{*}_{\equiv_{\lambda}}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}

and

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{N}^{\prime}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=Q^{\prime}
.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}\longrightarrow^{*}P_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus P_{k}
, and
P_{j}=P_{j}^{\prime\prime}\mid\checkmark
, for some
j
.

Notice that if
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}
is itself a term with unguarded
\checkmark
, say
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=P\mid\checkmark
, then
\mathbb{M}
is itself headed with
\checkmark
, from Proposition
27
(2).

In the case
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\llbracket}\mathbb{M}{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\rrbracket}^{{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\lightning}}_{u}=P_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus P_{k}
,
k\geq 2
, and
\checkmark
occurs unguarded in an
P_{j}
, The encoding acts homomorphically over sums and the reasoning is similar. We have that
P_{j}=P_{j}^{\prime}\mid\checkmark
we apply Proposition
27
(2).
